```text
3. **Root cause**: HuggingFace Hub HTTP client closed during a HEAD request to `https://huggingface.co/datasets/cais/mmlu/resolve/main/README.md`


4. **Retry mechanism failed**: The system attempted retries but ultimately couldn't recover from the connection drop.

This looks like a network or proxy issue on the server. The CLAUDE.md documentation warns about this exact scenario—I need to source the network turbo settings before any pip or git operations, set the SSL certificate environment variables, and disable XET for HuggingFace downloads. Looking at the reproduce.command in the registry, it seems these prerequisites weren't properly configured before the dataset loading attempt.

But here's what's puzzling me: the earlier MVP experiments (mvp_001, mvp_002, mvp_003) all successfully loaded the same dataset. Either the network_turbo was already sourced in the shell profile, or the dataset was already cached locally. The error itself is a HEAD request to HuggingFace that's timing out, which suggests the environment variables for SSL and HF caching weren't set up correctly this time around.

The real problem might be timing-related: the script loads the model first (about a minute total), and by the time it tries to fetch dataset metadata, the network proxy could have timed out or dropped the connection, leaving the httpx client in a closed state. For non-English languages, the script would need to download GlobalMMLU if it's not cached, which requires working network access.

The earlier MVP experiments likely had the dataset already cached locally, but the disk cleanup that removed old model files might have also cleared the HF cache. More likely though, the `submit_training_job` command runs in a tmux session that doesn't inherit the `network_turbo` environment variable from the original shell, so the network configuration isn't being applied properly.

This is the second failure now—the registry shows the job as RUNNING but the process has actually crashed with an exit code of 1. The real problem is that the launch command is missing the network configuration sourcing and the HF-specific environment variables needed for Hub access, plus it's not setting CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES even though the model did end up on GPU 0. I should verify whether the dataset is actually cached locally and adjust the script configuration accordingly.

There's also a path discrepancy I'm noticing—the traceback points to `/root/autodl-tmp/xling_calib_direction/code/extract_activations.py` but the registry command changes directory to `/root/xling_calib_direction`, which suggests either a symlink or a Python path resolution issue that's worth investigating.

The main issues to highlight are the HF Hub network error during dataset loading, the missing network configuration like `source /etc/network_turbo` and SSL environment variables, and several mitigation strategies: pre-caching the dataset, setting `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1`, adding network_turbo to the command, or configuring `HF_HOME` to avoid re-downloading.

The Qwen3 model download is nearly complete with 29 of 32 shards finished, though there are still 3 shards remaining in the temp directory taking up about 12GB. I should check whether the dataset might already be cached elsewhere on the system.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The dataset cache exists at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/datasets/cais___mmlu` (the new hub cache format uses `datasets/` not `datasets--`). 

But the error shows the script is trying to do a HEAD request to HuggingFace to get the README.md, which means it's not running in offline mode and is trying to verify/update the dataset. The dataset *is* cached, but the HF library still wants to check for updates.

The solution is:
1. Set `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1` so datasets library uses cache without network checks


2. OR source the network turbo config and set SSL environment variables to enable network requests
3. OR do both

I should also verify whether HF_HOME is properly configured in the environment. The dataset being cached at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/datasets/cais___mmlu` suggests HF_HOME was set to `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache` in earlier runs, but the current command doesn't specify it, so I need to check what the default HF_HOME would be and whether the script sets it internally.
```

_source line 1728 · 2026-05-21T11:56:23.346Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the picture:

1. `HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache` is set in `.bashrc`
2. The dataset is cached at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/datasets/cais___mmlu/all`
3. But the command runs via `bash -c "..."` which may not source `.bashrc` (non-interactive shell)

The `bash -c` invocation is a non-login, non-interactive shell. It sources:
- `/etc/bash.bashrc` (if Debian/Ubuntu) 
- NOT `~/.bashrc` (because that's only for interactive shells)


- So `HF_HOME` isn't set, the process defaults to `~/.cache/huggingface`, and the cached dataset at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/datasets/cais___mmlu/` isn't found. The script then tries downloading from HuggingFace but fails because the network configuration from `/etc/network_turbo` wasn't sourced either. The fix is to either explicitly export `HF_HOME` in the command or source the network configuration and SSL settings before running the script.

I should also consider using `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1` as a backup to prevent download attempts if the dataset is already cached. But there's another issue — the script loads English MMLU first, then non-English languages from GlobalMMLU, so both datasets need to be accessible or cached for plan_002 to work properly.

Now I'm putting together my findings for the report. This is a critical issue — plan_002 failed twice in a row, and the underlying problem is an environment configuration gap that will block all subsequent experiment runs. The core issue is that `bash -c` doesn't source `.bashrc`, so the `HF_HOME` variable pointing to the actual cache location never gets set, forcing the process to look in the default location and attempt downloads when the network proxy isn't configured. For the next retry, I need to explicitly export both `HF_HOME` and `HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE` directly in the command.

Beyond this immediate fix, the same `bash -c` pattern will cause identical failures when plan_001 launches, the registry is showing plan_002 as still running despite the process being dead, and I'm tracking that the Qwen3 model download is nearly complete with just three shards remaining. I should also note that the reproduce command is missing the `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0` specification, though the model did end up on the correct GPU anyway.
```

数据集缓存确认存在：`/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/datasets/cais___mmlu/all`\n  - `.bashrc` 确认有 `export HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache`，但 `bash -c` 不加载\n\n- [severity: critical] **这是系统性问题，会影响所有通过 `bash -c` 提交的实验**，包括即将启动的 plan_001。MVP 实验之所以没遇到，很可能是当时通过交互式 tmux session 手动启动（继承了 `.bashrc` 环境）。\n\n- [severity: warning] **Registry 状态不一致**：plan_002 显示 RUNNING 但进程已死。需手动更新为 FAILED。\n\n### 修复建议\n\n重试命令**必须**在 `python -u` 之前添加：\n```bash\nexport HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache && \\\nexport HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1 && \\\nexport CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 && \\\n```\n\n完整命令模板：\n```bash\nbash -c \"source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && \\\nexport PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 && \\\nexport HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache && \\\nexport HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1 && \\\nexport CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 && \\\ncd /root/xling_calib_direction && \\\npython -u code/extract_activations.py \\\n  --model_path /root/autodl-tmp/models/Llama4-Scout-W4A16/RedHatAI/Llama-4-Scout-17B-16E-Instruct-quantized___w4a16/ \\\n  --model_name llama4_scout_w4a16 \\\n  --layers 0,4,8,12,16,20,24,28,32,36,40,44,47 \\\n  --languages en zh ar hi sw ko es fr de ru tr bn ja pt vi te id pl nl uk \\\n  --output_dir /root/autodl-tmp/activations/llama4_scout/\"\n```\n\n**plan_001 启动时同样需要这些环境变量。**\n\n### 附：Qwen3 下载进度\n- 29/32 shards 完成，temp 目录剩 3 个（00027, 00029, 00031），约 12GB\n- GPUs 1-3 空闲，随时可启动 plan_001（下载完成后）",

_source line 1779 · 2026-05-21T11:58:16.941Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
plan_013_v2 progress:

| Layer | CV AUROC | Perm AUROC | Selectivity | Time |
|-------|----------|------------|-------------|------|
| 0 | 0.6304 | 0.5007 | +0.1297 | 12719s (~3.5h) |
| 12 | 0.6389 | 0.4977 | +0.1411 | 9291s (~2.6h) |
| 16 | 0.6801 | 0.5038 | +0.1764 | 7605s (~2.1h) |


| 20 | 0.7211 | 0.5006 | +0.2205 | 7678s (~2.1h) |

Four layers completed so far with the remaining ones being 24, 27, 4, and 8. The trend is clear—deeper layers are yielding stronger AUROC scores and higher selectivity gaps, with layer 20 reaching 0.7211 AUROC and a selectivity advantage of 0.22. Now comparing these results against the original plan_013 approach using PCA and lbfgs to see how the liblinear variant without PCA stacks up.

Looking at the side-by-side metrics, the liblinear version without PCA is outperforming the PCA approach at shallower layers—layer 0 jumps from 0.501 to 0.6304, and layer 12 improves from 0.530 to 0.6389—but the advantage narrows at deeper layers where the original PCA method actually pulls ahead slightly at layer 20.

The selectivity pattern is revealing too: v2 shows meaningful selectivity even at the shallowest layer (0.13 versus 0.02), which contradicts the earlier assumption that selectivity concentrates only in the deeper layers. That 8% of variance discarded by PCA at L0 appears to contain signal that matters for the classifier, whereas the full-dimensional features in v2 can capture these subtler patterns through regularization.

I need to be cautious about drawing conclusions though, since I've changed two things at once—removing PCA and switching solvers—so I can't cleanly isolate which change drives the difference. What's more important for the probing analysis is that both versions confirm the real features outperform permuted ones, and v2's more gradual selectivity increase across layers suggests a different story than plan_013's sharp jump at L16-L20. I should wait for all eight layers before making a final call on what this means for the paper's narrative.

Now I'm checking the overall project status and looking at what else needs attention. The CKA pilot data is already complete for the MVP with Qwen2.5-7B, so I want to cross-reference it with the transfer AUROC results to see if Simpson's Paradox shows up in the data. The layer-level transfer AUROC from mvp_001 isn't fully documented, but I can use the best_zs_auroc values to identify which layer performed best per language and dig deeper from there.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have detailed layer-by-layer AUROC data from summary.txt. Let me compile the CKA vs transfer AUROC correlation.

From summary.txt (zero_shot AUROC per layer per language):

| Layer | zh AUROC | ar AUROC | hi AUROC | ko AUROC | sw AUROC |
|-------|----------|----------|----------|----------|----------|
| 0 | 0.517 | 0.502 | 0.548 | 0.500 | 0.514 |


| 4 | 0.573 | 0.521 | 0.559 | 0.518 | 0.519 |
| 8 | 0.525 | 0.523 | 0.427 | 0.498 | 0.487 |
| 12 | 0.562 | 0.513 | 0.448 | 0.486 | 0.486 |
| 16 | 0.597 | 0.589 | 0.457 | 0.573 | 0.532 |
| 20 | 0.666 | 0.643 | 0.652 | 0.627 | 0.544 |
| 24 | 0.686 | 0 |

Generating AUROC table... Generating markdown table...

I'm noticing a discrepancy — the summary shows L27 results for English but the per-language breakdowns only go through L24, which seems inconsistent with the probing configuration that includes layer 27. Let me verify whether L27 data actually exists for all languages or if there's a reporting issue in the summary file. Looking at the Arabic data in the summary, I see layers 0 through 24 tested with the best AUROC of 0.651 at L24, but the spec shows L27 was also tested—so either the summary didn't capture that result or there's a discrepancy in what got recorded.

With the available L0-L24 data across all languages, I have enough points to examine the Simpson's Paradox pattern: CKA decreases as we go deeper while transfer AUROC improves, suggesting a negative correlation overall that contradicts what we might expect within individual language groups.

Now checking within each layer to see if languages with higher CKA consistently show higher transfer AUROC. At L0, the pattern breaks down—Hindi has the lowest CKA but the highest AUROC, while Mandarin has the highest CKA but only middle-range AUROC, indicating the within-layer relationship doesn't follow the same trend as the across-layer pattern.

Moving to L16, the picture clarifies: Mandarin and Arabic both show high CKA paired with high AUROC, while Hindi drops to low CKA with the lowest AUROC, and Swahili sits in the middle for both metrics. This suggests a positive correlation emerging at deeper layers. By L24, Mandarin again leads in both metrics, and the languages with the lowest CKA values also have the lowest AUROC scores, reinforcing the positive trend, though Arabic's position slightly complicates the pattern.

What's happening here is Simpson's Paradox—depth is the confounding variable, independently pushing CKA downward while pulling AUROC upward across layers. Once I account for depth, the true positive relationship between CKA and AUROC becomes visible. The within-layer analysis is promising, though I should be cautious: with only five languages per layer, the correlations are statistically fragile, and the pattern isn't consistent across all layers like L0. The upcoming analysis with twenty languages will give me much stronger power to detect this relationship reliably.

Before I commit to any conclusions, I need to wait for those larger results. For now, let me check what else I have—the CKA pilot worker file was enormous but that's just the raw session log; the actual clean results are in the CSV I already pulled. Let me look at the transfer results file to see if there's anything else worth examining.
```

CKA Pilot 初步分析 — Simpson's Paradox 信号明确\n\n[severity: info] 交叉对比 `cka_results.csv` × `transfer_results.csv` (Qwen2.5-7B, 5 langs × 7 layers):\n\n**边际关系（跨层 pooled）**: 负相关 ✓\n- 深层 CKA 急剧下降 (zh: L0=0.91 → L27=0.34; sw: L0=0.44 → L27=0.08)\n- 深层 transfer AUROC 上升 (zh: L0=0.52 → L24=0.69; ar: L0=0.50 → L24=0.65)\n- 这正是 mvp_003 报告的 overall ρ=-0.40\n\n**层内关系（控制 depth 后）**: 正信号出现 ✓\n- L16: zh(CKA=0.89/AUROC=0.60), ar(0.88/0.59), ko(0.87/0.57), sw(0.59/0.53), hi(0.58/0.46) — CKA 排序 ≈ AUROC 排序\n- L24: zh(0.69/0.69), ko(0.55/0.65), ar(0.54/0.65), sw(0.13/0.53), hi(0.16/0.52) — hi/sw 的低 CKA 对应低 AUROC\n- 浅层（L0-L4）: AUROC 接近随机（0.50-0.55），区分力不足，相关信号弱\n\n**结论**：pilot 数据与 Simpson's Paradox 假设一致——depth 是混淆变量，同时驱动 CKA↓ 和 AUROC↑，掩盖了层内 CKA→AUROC 的正向关系。\n\n**但 n=5 统计功效不足**。每层仅 5 个语言点，Spearman 相关在 n=5 下不可靠。plan_001/002 的 20 语言数据将提供 4 倍功效，是决定性验证。\n\n### 3. plan_013_v2 vs plan_013 对比\n\n[severity: warning] v2 与 v1 数值差异显著，影响叙事方向。\n\n| 层 | v1 (PCA+lbfgs) AUROC/Sel | v2 (no PCA+liblinear) AUROC/Sel |\n|------|------|------|\n| L0 | 0.501 / 0.02 | 0.630 / 0.13 |\n| L12 | 0.530 / 0.04 | 0.639 / 0.14 |\n| L16 | 0.615 / 0.12 | 0.680 / 0.18 |\n| L20 | 0.733 / 0.24 | 0.721 / 0.22 |\n\n- v1 叙事：\"selectivity 集中在 L20+\" — L0 selectivity 仅 0.02\n- v2 数据：L0 selectivity 已达 0.13，不算\"集中在深层\"\n\n**原因**：两个变量同时变了（PCA 和 solver），无法分离影响。但 v2 更接近实际 P0 pipeline（liblinear、无 PCA），应以 v2 为准。\n\n**建议**：等 v2 全部 8 层完成后，以 v2 结果定稿 probing controls 叙事。L24/L27 数据待出，如果 selectivity 在深层仍有明显增量（从 0.22 → 0.30+），\"depth-dependent selectivity\" 仍可讲，只是用词需从 \"concentrated in L20+\" 调整为 \"monotonically increasing with depth\"。",

Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Multiple findings here:

1. **plan_002 (PID 477148) is dead again** — fourth failure! This time the error is completely different:
   - `torch.OutOfMemoryError: CUDA out of memory` on GPU 0
   - "GPU 0 has a total capacity of 94.97 GiB of which 25.81 MiB is free"
   - Process tried to allocate 160 MB but GPU 0 had 94.94 GiB in use
   
   But wait — the model was loading (decompressing W4A16 weights). 

The issue is that decompressing from int4 to the full representation temporarily requires nearly double the memory, so the ~61GB compressed model needs over 96GB during decompression on a single GPU.

2. **GPU Status NOW**: 
   - GPU 0: 3 MiB (empty — plan_002 crashed and freed memory)
   - GPU 1: 90149 MiB (~88GB used!)
   - GPU 2: 93611 MiB (~91.4GB used!)
   - GPU 3: 93611 MiB (~91.4GB used!)

   Something substantial is loaded on GPUs 1-3.

3. **plan_001 is running**: Qwen3-235B-GPTQ-Int4 is now active, with the ~126GB compressed model distributed across the three GPUs.

4. **plan_002 OOM root cause**: The Llama4 Scout W4A16 model uses compressed_tensors quantization, and when the forward pass triggers the decompression hook, it tries to unpack int4 weights into larger formats in-place on the GPU. The compressed weights fit within the 96GB card, but the decompression process itself consumed 93.27 GiB allocated plus 1.04 GiB reserved, totaling 94.31 GiB and exceeding the available 94.97 GiB. The fundamental problem is that W4A16 decompression requires more memory than the compressed model alone.

5. **Investigating why retry 1 succeeded**: Looking back at the earlier logs, retry 1 actually loaded the model successfully — it showed the 48-layer architecture with hidden_dim=5120 and started processing the first language sample before hitting a Hub error. But attempt 4 crashed with OOM despite similar conditions. Now I'm noticing that GPUs 1-3 are each showing 88-91GB of memory usage, which suggests plan_001 (Qwen3) might still be occupying significant space.

6. **Checking the actual command configuration**: The latest command in the registry spec is missing the `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0` constraint that should isolate plan_002 to GPU 0. I'm examining the full command to see what environment variables are actually being set and whether GPU isolation is properly configured.

7. **Analyzing device allocation with all GPUs visible**: Since the script uses `--device_map auto` by default and all 4 GPUs are visible to the process, it should place the model on GPU 0 (the only free GPU while Qwen3 occupies GPUs 1-3). But the error message indicates GPU 0 is nearly full, which suggests something went wrong with the memory allocation or the decompression process consumed more space than expected.

8. **Comparing retry 1 vs attempt 4**: The key difference is that retry 1 successfully completed the "Compressing model" step at 100% before loading weights, while attempt 4 failed partway through "Decompressing model" at 29%, suggesting the decompression is where the memory spike occurs and exceeds the 95GB capacity. the memory issue. The key difference seems to be that with `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1`, the model uses lazy decompression during inference rather than pre-decompressing everything upfront, which is why it ran out of memory on the first forward pass instead of completing at least one sample like in retry 1.

But wait — I just noticed that `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0` isn't actually set in the command, so with `device_map="auto"`, the model could be spreading across all available GPUs instead of staying on GPU 0, which might explain the OOM if there's a conflict with the other plan loading simultaneously.

Actually, the real issue is probably simpler: even if the model lands entirely on GPU 0 because GPUs 1-3 are full, the W4A16 format expands significantly during decompression. The compressed ~61GB balloons to ~95GB or more as int4 weights unpack to int8, and a 95GB GPU doesn't have enough headroom for that expansion.

But wait—retry 1 actually succeeded in loading and started processing samples before hitting the HF Hub error. So the model did fit on one GPU at some point. The key difference might be how `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` affects decompression: without it, weights decompress once during loading; with it enabled, the `compressed_tensors` library might decompress lazily during inference, requiring both compressed and decompressed versions in memory simultaneously, which triggers the OOM.

Looking at the actual memory numbers from the error—93.27 GiB allocated plus 1.04 GiB reserved—the compressed weights around 61GB are ballooning to 93+ GB with decompression overhead. For Llama4 Scout W4A16 with 109B parameters (17B active per token in MoE), the int4 quantized weights need to dequantize to float16 during inference, and that expansion is consuming nearly all available VRAM.

The real culprit seems to be the `compressed_tensors` library's decompression hook—it's decompressing the entire model at once on the first forward pass rather than layer-by-layer, which means it needs memory for both the compressed and decompressed weights simultaneously.

Let me recalculate the memory requirements: the int4 weights alone are around 6.8GB, but with grouping and scales in safetensors format it's closer to 61GB, while full float16 decompression would need 218GB—clearly impossible on a single GPU. Looking at the traceback, the `unpack_from_int32` function is unpacking int4 values from int32 containers, creating intermediate tensors that are adding to the memory pressure.

The actual OOM error shows it failed trying to allocate just 160MB, which means the model was already consuming ~95GB on GPU 0. So during the first forward pass, the decompression hook attempted to decompress weights but ran out of memory—this is the fourth failure in a retry sequence. Interestingly, the same model loaded and ran inference successfully in an earlier retry, so something about the current state is different.

Now I'm noticing that GPUs 1-3 are heavily occupied with ~90GB each, which suggests plan_001 may have launched—possibly even a Qwen3 model. The command still lacks CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0, so I need to verify what's actually running on those GPUs and confirm plan_002 has a fundamental memory constraint. The core issue is that plan_002 needs at least two GPUs with device_map="auto" to fit, but GPUs 1-3 are already taken by what looks like plan_001.

For the next attempt, I could try 4-bit quantization via bitsandbytes instead of the current compression method, enable expandable memory segments in PyTorch, or explicitly restrict to GPU 0 with a smaller footprint. But here's the key insight: retry 1 actually succeeded, which means the model can fit on a single GPU under the right conditions—the difference might be in how the model decompresses or loads when offline mode is enabled. I should just document this finding and move forward.
```

````text
It's a dry run of plan_001! The command is:
```
python -u code/extract_activations.py 
  --model_path /root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen3-235B-GPTQ-Int4/ 
  --model_name qwen3_235b_gptq 
  --layers 0,4 
  --languages en zh 
  --output_dir /root/autodl-tmp/activations/plan_001_dryrun 
  --max_samples 5


```

The dry run is testing with minimal scope—just 2 layers, 2 languages, and 5 samples—and the Qwen3-235B-GPTQ-Int4 model is distributed across 3 GPUs totaling around 277 GB. The process started at 19:58, and it's using GPUs 1, 2, and 3, though I notice CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES isn't explicitly set in the command, so it might actually be seeing all 4 GPUs available.

Looking at the memory allocation, GPU 0 is nearly empty while GPUs 1-3 each have roughly 90-94 GB allocated, which suggests the auto device mapping placed the model there—possibly because GPU 0 was already occupied or came later in the allocation order. The total ~273 GB loaded is notably higher than the expected ~126 GB compressed size for this model, which makes sense given the decompression overhead during loading.

```
Qwen3-235B is a MoE model with 235B active parameters, and with 128 experts (8 active per token) across 94 layers, the total parameter count is substantial. At GPTQ-Int4 compression, the model file is around 134 GB, but the decompression during loading inflates this significantly—the 273 GB across the three GPUs aligns with what I'd expect for a model of this scale with decompression overhead.
```

Now I need to check the dry run logs to see if it's actually progressing or if it's stuck somewhere.
````

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I see the full picture:

1. **The dryrun.log** shows a SUCCESSFUL Llama4 Scout dry run! Key details:
   - Decompression completed 100% (5076/5076 items)
   - `[en]: 100%` — 1 batch of 1 completed in 20.12s
   - "en: N=2, train=1, eval=1, acc=0.500, shape=(2, 5120), time=20.2s"
   - Output: `/root/xling_calib_direction/activations/plan_002_dryrun/llama4_scout_w4a16`
   - 13 layers extracted, 0.3 MB, 66s total

2. **The run_dryrun.sh** script leverages all 4 GPUs and sets up the necessary environment variables for Hugging Face datasets and models, though it looks like the script definition got cut off mid-way.

3. **PID 482839** is running a Qwen3-235B model extraction, which means the Llama4 Scout dry run has already finished and its results are logged, while the Agent is now processing a different model.

The Llama4 dry run consumed all four GPUs, which is why the plan_002 retry that was submitted later ran into out-of-memory issues on GPU 0 — both processes were competing for the same resources. 3 of plan_002 at 11:58, but that also failed since the dry run revealed the model actually needs 4 GPUs for decompression, not just 1. The dry run succeeded with all 4 GPUs available, while plan_002 attempt 4 only had 1 GPU and ran out of memory at 29% decompression. So the real constraint is figuring out how to allocate GPUs between plan_002 (Llama4 Scout) and plan_001 (Qwen3) when both require multiple GPUs.

Looking at the GPU allocation more carefully, the Llama4 dry run used all 4 GPUs during decompression, but after it completed, GPU 0 freed up while Qwen3 started using GPUs 1-3 with 273 GiB allocated. This means they ran sequentially rather than in parallel, with Qwen3 taking the remaining GPUs after Llama4 finished. The Qwen3 dry run is currently consuming 3 GPUs with 273 GiB, and since Llama4 also needs multiple GPUs for decompression, they can't run simultaneously—this is fundamentally a scheduling conflict where these models must execute sequentially. The W4A16 compressed model requires significant temporary memory overhead during decompression beyond just its compressed size, so even with 2 GPUs available, I need to verify whether that's actually sufficient for the decompression step.

I'm weighing several approaches: running them back-to-back with Qwen3 first, trying the expandable segments configuration to reduce memory fragmentation, using 2 GPUs for Llama4 while restricting Qwen3 to the remaining GPUs, or switching to bitsandbytes for model loading instead. Let me check whether the previous retry attempt included the memory optimization flag.

Looking at the latest command spec, it's missing the `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF` setting that was in the dry run script, so adding that memory optimization plus restricting to a single GPU might be the fix. But the dry run used 4 GPUs and still succeeded, so I'm not certain it would work with just 1 GPU—the model might genuinely need more than 95GB. The core issue is that Llama4 in compressed_tensors format appears to require more than a single GPU can provide.

This means the two plans can't run in parallel on the available hardware and will need to execute sequentially instead.
```

```text
Now I have THREE versions of CKA data:

1. **cka_results.csv** (May 21 04:22) — first run
2. **cka_matrix_verify.csv** (May 21 10:50) — verification run  
3. **cka_pilot/cka_matrix.csv** (May 21 20:00) — latest pilot run

Let me compare the values:

| Layer | Lang | cka_results.csv | cka_matrix_verify.csv | cka_pilot/cka_matrix.csv |
|-------|------|----------------|----------------------|--------------------------|


| 0 | zh | 0.912057 | 0.912092 | 0.959179 |
| 0 | ar | 0.901368 | 0.901320 | 0.948072 |
| 0 | hi | 0.536453 | 0.536285 | 0.745915 |
| 0 | sw | 0.439737 | 0.439818 | 0.905768 |
| 0 | ko | 0.686871 | 0.686777 | 0.793891 |
| 16 | zh | 0.885897 | 0.885929 | 0.910601 |
| 16 | sw | 0.592 |

The pilot run shows significant improvements across most languages, especially for Hindi and Swahili at layer 0, while the first two runs are nearly identical to each other. The discrepancies between the pilot and original results are wildly inconsistent—some layers barely shift while others jump dramatically, which suggests either different activation data or a fundamental difference in how the CKA computation itself is being performed. The first two implementations agree with each other down to minor decimal differences, so that's consistent — but the pilot run produces substantially different values, especially for low-scoring language-layer pairs where differences reach up to 0.47. The pattern suggests the pilot might be using the full activation dataset rather than a subset, which would explain why shallow layers show the most dramatic changes while deep layers remain relatively stable.

I need to verify whether the pilot is actually running the same compute_cka.py script or if something else generated those results.
```

### Assistant

_source line 1929 · 2026-05-21T12:06:27.573Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I have the full picture:

**PID 322679 command**:
```
python code/compute_cka.py \
  --activation_dir activations \
  --model_name qwen25-7b-mvp \
  --metrics_dir results \
  --probes_dir probes \
  --output_dir results/qwen25-7b-mvp/cka_pilot \
  --n_bootstrap 1000 \
  --seed 42
```

But the output went to `results/cka_pilot/cka_matrix.csv` (according to earlier `find` results). 

There's a mismatch here—the command specifies `results/qwen25-7b-mvp/cka_pilot` as the output directory, but I found the file at `results/cka_pilot/cka_matrix.csv`. Either the script isn't using the `--output_dir` argument as expected, or there are actually two separate outputs being written to different locations.

The real puzzle is why the CKA values are so different between the two files. It could be that they're using different activation data—the script points to a local `activations` directory rather than the one in `/root/autodl-tmp/`—or they were generated by different versions of the compute script entirely, or possibly one used the full dataset while the other used only the eval split.

Looking at the timestamps, `cka_results.csv` was created very early in the project (04:22), while the current `compute_cka.py` script has gone through multiple iterations. The earlier version likely had different data processing logic—maybe no train/eval split, different centering, or other preprocessing differences. Since I can't determine which version is actually correct without diving into the code history, the safest approach is to flag this discrepancy and recommend using only the latest results from the current script.

Comparing the layer-by-layer results...

Generating comparison table...

The discrepancies cluster around low-CKA language pairs at shallow layers, with the biggest gaps appearing early in the network. This points to a centering difference—centered CKA inflates scores for dissimilar representations since it removes the mean contribution, which could explain why the new results are consistently higher. Another possibility is that the data split changed, where using only the eval set instead of all data introduces different variance properties in the CKA measurements.

Either way, I need to resolve this data integrity issue before relying on these CKA values for the paper or the Simpson's Paradox analysis.
````

_source line 1946 · 2026-05-21T12:07:32.720Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is the CUDA OOM failure I already identified and reported. The plan_002 has now been formally marked as failed by the cron hook. The error is identical to what I analyzed before:

- `ct_decompress_hook` triggered on first forward pass
- `unpack_from_int32` tried to allocate 160 MiB but GPU 0 was at 94.94/94.97 GiB
- EXIT_CODE=1

The registry now shows plan_002 is NOT in the RUNNING list (only plan_013_v2 is). So plan_002 has been properly updated to a non-RUNNING state.


I've already covered this comprehensively with Director—the Llama4 Scout W4A16 model requires over 95 GiB for decompression on a single GPU, the 4-GPU dry run confirmed it works, and there's a fundamental scheduling conflict where Qwen3 needs 3 GPUs while Llama4 needs 2+, making simultaneous execution impossible on 4 GPUs. The cron hook has now formally captured the failure, so I just need to verify the registry status reflects this correctly.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me analyze the ROADMAP update:

**Key change**: GPU scheduling revised due to plan_002 OOM. New plan:
- Llama4 Scout: cuda:0,1 (2 GPUs instead of 1)
- Qwen3-235B: cuda:2,3 (2 GPUs instead of 3)

**Issues to review**:

1. **Llama4 on 2 GPUs**: The dry run succeeded with 4 GPUs. The single-GPU attempt needed 94.94 GiB out of 94.97 GiB. 

Doubling to 2 GPUs gives 190 GiB total, which should provide enough buffer for decompression overhead during loading.

2. **Qwen3 on 2 GPUs**: The original 3-GPU plan (cuda:1,2,3) showed ~273 GiB total usage across the dry run (90+94+94 GiB). Cutting this down to just 2 GPUs (190 GiB) is a significant reduction and will almost certainly cause an OOM error.

   Actually, reconsidering this — the GPTQ-Int4 compressed model is around 134 GiB, and the MarlinLinear kernel handles int4→fp16 dequantization on-the-fly during the forward pass rather than decompressing everything upfront. So the stored weights stay at 134 GiB (int4 packed), leaving roughly 56 GiB of headroom on 2 GPUs for KV cache, activations, and other overhead — which is likely insufficient.

   The ROADMAP's estimate of ~117GB for Qwen3 might be based on compressed size without accounting for runtime buffers, or it's using a different calculation method. Meanwhile, the GPU budget assumes 8 GPUs in parallel for 25 wall-clock hours, but westd-13176 only has 4 GPUs available right now, cutting the effective parallelism in half. And from the MoE lesson, I need to remember that for models with mixture-of-experts, total parameters matter for memory estimation, not just active parameters.

   Llama4 Scout illustrates this perfectly—it has 109B total params but only 17B active per token, yet the compressed model still stores all expert weights. The scheduling risk is real: if Qwen3 needs 3 GPUs and Llama4 needs 2, I can't run them simultaneously on 4 GPUs. The ROADMAP acknowledges this with a fallback plan—start with 2 GPUs for Qwen3, and if that runs out of memory, wait for the first phase to finish and use 3-4 GPUs instead. That's a pragmatic approach to the constraint.

   There's also a CKA data inconsistency I flagged that isn't addressed in the ROADMAP, and plan_013_v2 is still running—though the ROADMAP does call out the selectivity threshold sensitivity work as a near-term task once it completes.

   Looking at the overall picture, the ROADMAP revision is mostly sound, but there's one significant concern: Qwen3-235B on just 2 GPUs is likely to hit out-of-memory errors. The dry run showed 277 GiB of total GPU usage spread across 3 GPUs, which means with only 2 GPUs and 190 GiB available, I'd have just 56 GiB of headroom for KV cache, activations, and PyTorch overhead. The lightest GPU in that run used 90 GiB alone, so unless the model can compress substantially, 2 GPUs won't be enough.

   The fallback—waiting for plan_002 to finish and then using 3-4 GPUs—makes sense, but it means plan_001 and plan_002 will run back-to-back instead of in parallel, effectively doubling the wall clock time. The 117 GiB estimate for Qwen3 also seems optimistic compared to what the dry run actually consumed, and the GPU-hours projection assumes both servers are available with concurrent execution, which won't happen if we're forced into sequential mode on just westd-13176.
```

_source line 2048 · 2026-05-21T12:11:35.889Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I see the full picture of plan_002_4gpu's failure:

**Root cause**: The Qwen3 dry run (PID 482839) was still occupying GPUs 1-3 when plan_002_4gpu was launched on all 4 GPUs!

Key evidence from the error:
```
GPU 1 has a total capacity of 94.97 GiB of which 32.50 MiB is free. 


```

- **Process 482839** (Qwen3 dry run) is consuming 88.03 GiB on GPU 1, leaving only 6.90 GiB for plan_002_4gpu's process
- GPU 1 was 99.97% full → OOM trying to allocate 40 MiB

So plan_002_4gpu was launched with `device_map=auto` while the previous process was still hogging the GPUs.

The real issue here is that when plan_002_4gpu tried to distribute across all 4 GPUs, GPUs 1-3 were already occupied by PID 482839 (the Qwen3 dry run using ~88-91 GiB each). The device_map logic placed some Llama4 layers on GPU 1, but ran out of memory during decompression. This is a resource management failure—the Qwen3 dry run should have been terminated before launching plan_002_4gpu, especially since it was just a test run with limited samples and specific layer configurations.

Looking at my L1 constraints more carefully, this isn't technically a cross-project GPU boundary violation since both processes belong to the same project. It's more of an intra-project scheduling conflict where the Agent didn't properly clean up or check for existing GPU occupants before launching a new job that required all 4 devices.
````

</details>

````json
{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — plan_002_4gpu FAILED\n\n**结论**: CRITICAL（资源管理失误）\n\n### 失败根因\n\n[severity: critical] **Agent 在 Qwen3 dry run (PID 482839) 仍占用 GPU 1-3 的情况下启动了需要 4 GPU 的 plan_002_4gpu，导致 OOM。**\n\n错误日志明确指出：\n```\nGPU 1: Process 482839 has 88.03 GiB memory in use.\nThis process (plan_002_4gpu) has 6.90 GiB memory in use.\nTried to allocate 40 MiB → OOM\n```\n\n**时间线**：\n1. PID 482839 (Qwen3 dry run, `--max_samples 5`) 启动 → 占用 GPU 1-3 (~273 GiB)\n2. Agent 创建 plan_002_4gpu (需 4 GPU) 并提交\n3. `device_map=auto` 将 Llama4 分布到 GPU 0-3\n4. GPU 1 上 Qwen3 占了 88 GiB + Llama4 分配的 6.9 GiB → 第一次 forward pass 时 OOM\n\n### 问题本质\n\n**Agent 未在启动前清理 GPU。** Qwen3 dry run 只是测试运行（5 samples, 2 layers），已无保留价值，应在提交 plan_002_4gpu 之前 kill。\n\n这不是 GPU 白名单越界（两个进程都是本项目的），而是**项目内调度冲突**——同一组 GPU 被两个进程争抢。\n\n### 修复路径\n\n1. **先 kill PID 482839**（Qwen3 dry run），释放 GPU 1-3\n2. 确认 4 GPU 全部空闲后再提交 plan_002_4gpu\n3. 日后每次提交前应执行 `nvidia-smi` 检查 GPU 占用\n\n### 数据影响\n\nplan_002_4gpu 的 `en` 数据集已从离线缓存成功加载（\"Using the latest cached version\"），说明 HF_HOME + HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE=1 修复有效。OOM 发生在模型解压阶段，与数据无关。\n\n### 附注\n\n这是 Llama4 Scout extraction 的**第 5 次失败**（plan_002 × 4 次 + plan_002_4gpu × 1 次）。每次失败根因不同（argparse → HF网络 → 单GPU OOM → 单GPU OOM重复 → 多GPU被占），但都是**可预防的操作失误**。建议 Agent 建立 pre-flight checklist：\n1. ✅ 参数正确（--model_name, --languages 空格分隔）\n2. ✅ 环境变量完整（HF_HOME, OFFLINE, PYTHONUNBUFFERED）\n3. ✅ GPU 空闲（nvidia-smi 无其他进程）\n4. ✅ 磁盘空间充足（df -h）",
````
